SQL PRACTICE PACK — TABLES PER QUESTION
========================================================================
Each question is followed immediately by only the table(s) relevant to that question,
then the question and the answer from the source PDF. No CREATE TABLE or INSERT DDL.
Sample rows are illustrative. Some advanced questions require columns or tables that
the source PDF mentions but does not fully define; those are marked explicitly.
SQL dialect varies in the source PDF; validate syntax for your database.


========================================================================
QUESTION ITEM 001 (PDF NUMBER 1)
QUESTION: Find the second highest salary from the Employee table.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT MAX(salary) AS SecondHighestSalary
   FROM employees
   WHERE salary < (
   SELECT MAX(salary)
   FROM employees
   );

QUESTION ITEM 002 (PDF NUMBER 2)
QUESTION: Find duplicate records in a table.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, COUNT(*)
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY name
   HAVING COUNT(*) > 1;

QUESTION ITEM 003 (PDF NUMBER 3)
QUESTION: Retrieve employees who earn more than their manager.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name AS Employee, e.salary, m.name AS
   Manager, m.salary AS ManagerSalary
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE e.salary > m.salary;

QUESTION ITEM 004 (PDF NUMBER 4)
QUESTION: Count employees in each department having more than 5 employees.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, COUNT(*) AS
   num_employees
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   HAVING COUNT(*) > 5;

QUESTION ITEM 005 (PDF NUMBER 5)
QUESTION: Find employees who joined in the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM employees
   WHERE join_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 months';

QUESTION ITEM 006 (PDF NUMBER 6)
QUESTION: Get departments with no employees.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT d.department_name
   FROM departments d
   LEFT JOIN employees e ON d.department_id =
   e.department_id
   WHERE e.id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 007 (PDF NUMBER 7)
QUESTION: Write a query to find the median salary.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT AVG(salary) AS median_salary

    FROM (
   SELECT salary
   FROM employees
   ORDER BY salary
   LIMIT 2 - (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM employees)
   %2
   OFFSET (SELECT (COUNT(*) - 1) / 2 FROM
   employees)
   ) AS median_subquery;

QUESTION ITEM 008 (PDF NUMBER 8)
QUESTION: Running total of salaries by department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, department_id, salary,
   SUM(salary) OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id ORDER BY id) AS running_total
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 009 (PDF NUMBER 9)
QUESTION: Find the longest consecutive streak of daily logins for each user.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: user_logins
COLUMNS: user_id | login_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | 2025-01-01
  101 | 2025-01-02
  101 | 2025-01-03
  102 | 2025-01-01
  102 | 2025-01-03

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH login_dates AS (
   SELECT user_id, login_date,
   login_date - INTERVAL ROW_NUMBER()
   OVER (PARTITION BY user_id ORDER BY
   login_date) DAY AS grp
   FROM user_logins

   )
   SELECT user_id, COUNT(*) AS streak_length,
   MIN(login_date) AS start_date, MAX(login_date) AS
   end_date
   FROM login_dates
   GROUP BY user_id, grp

     ORDER BY streak_length DESC;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 010 (PDF NUMBER 10)
QUESTION: Recursive query to find the full reporting chain for each employee.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE reporting_chain AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id, 1 AS level
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id IS NULL
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id, rc.level + 1
   FROM employees e
   JOIN reporting_chain rc ON e.manager_id = rc.id
   )
   SELECT * FROM reporting_chain ORDER BY level,
   id;

QUESTION ITEM 011 (PDF NUMBER 11)
QUESTION: Write a query to find gaps in a sequence of numbers (missing IDs).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT (id + 1) AS missing_id
   FROM employees e1
   WHERE NOT EXISTS (
   SELECT 1 FROM employees e2 WHERE e2.id =
   e1.id + 1
   )
   ORDER BY missing_id;

QUESTION ITEM 012 (PDF NUMBER 12)
QUESTION: Calculate cumulative distribution (CDF) of salaries.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, salary,

    CUME_DIST() OVER (ORDER BY salary) AS
   salary_cdf
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 013 (PDF NUMBER 13)
QUESTION: Compare two tables and find rows with differences in any column (all columns).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM table1 t1
   FULL OUTER JOIN table2 t2 ON t1.id = t2.id
   WHERE t1.col1 IS DISTINCT FROM t2.col1
   OR t1.col2 IS DISTINCT FROM t2.col2
   OR t1.col3 IS DISTINCT FROM t2.col3;

QUESTION ITEM 014 (PDF NUMBER 14)
QUESTION: Write a query to rank employees based on salary with ties handled properly.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, salary,
   RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS
   salary_rank
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 015 (PDF NUMBER 15)
QUESTION: Find customers who have not made any purchase.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT c.customer_id, c.name
   FROM customers c
   LEFT JOIN sales s ON c.customer_id = s.customer_id
   WHERE s.sale_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 016 (PDF NUMBER 16)
QUESTION: Write a query to perform a conditional aggregation (count males and females in each

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
department).
   SELECT department_id,

    COUNT(CASE WHEN gender = 'M' THEN 1
   END) AS male_count,
   COUNT(CASE WHEN gender = 'F' THEN 1
   END) AS female_count
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 017 (PDF NUMBER 17)
QUESTION: Write a query to calculate the difference between current row and previous row's salary (lag

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
function).
   SELECT name, salary,
   salary - LAG(salary) OVER (ORDER BY id) AS
   salary_diff
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 018 (PDF NUMBER 18)
QUESTION: Identify overlapping date ranges for bookings.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: bookings
COLUMNS: booking_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 2025-01-01 | 2025-01-10
  2 | 2025-01-05 | 2025-01-15
  3 | 2025-02-01 | 2025-02-03

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT b1.booking_id, b2.booking_id
   FROM bookings b1
   JOIN bookings b2 ON b1.booking_id <> b2.booking_id
   WHERE b1.start_date <= b2.end_date
   AND b1.end_date >= b2.start_date;

QUESTION ITEM 019 (PDF NUMBER 19)
QUESTION: Write a query to find employees with salary greater than average salary in the entire

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
company, ordered by salary descending.
   SELECT name, salary
   FROM employees

    WHERE salary > (SELECT AVG(salary) FROM
   employees)
   ORDER BY salary DESC;

QUESTION ITEM 020 (PDF NUMBER 20)
QUESTION: Aggregate JSON data (if supported) to list all employee names in a department as a JSON

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
array.
   SELECT department_id, JSON_AGG(name) AS
   employee_names
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 021 (PDF NUMBER 21)
QUESTION: Find employees who have the same salary as their manager.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name AS Employee, e.salary, m.name AS
   Manager
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE e.salary = m.salary;

QUESTION ITEM 022 (PDF NUMBER 22)
QUESTION: Write a query to get the first and last purchase date for each customer.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id,
   MIN(purchase_date) AS first_purchase,
   MAX(purchase_date) AS last_purchase
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY customer_id;

QUESTION ITEM 023 (PDF NUMBER 23)
QUESTION: Find departments with the highest average salary.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_salaries AS (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT *
   FROM avg_salaries
   WHERE avg_salary = (SELECT MAX(avg_salary)
   FROM avg_salaries);

QUESTION ITEM 026 (PDF NUMBER 21)
QUESTION: Write a query to find the difference in days between two dates in the same table.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT id, DATEDIFF(day, start_date, end_date) AS
   days_difference
   FROM projects;

    Note: DATEDIFF syntax varies - replace accordingly
   (e.g., DATEDIFF('day', start_date, end_date) in some
   systems).

QUESTION ITEM 027 (PDF NUMBER 22)
QUESTION: Calculate the moving average of salaries over the last 3 employees ordered by hire date.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, hire_date, salary,
   AVG(salary) OVER (ORDER BY hire_date
   ROWS BETWEEN 2 PRECEDING AND CURRENT
   ROW) AS moving_avg_salary
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 028 (PDF NUMBER 23)
QUESTION: Find the most recent purchase per customer using window functions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM (
   SELECT *,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY purchase_date DESC) AS rn
   FROM sales
   ) sub
   WHERE rn = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 029 (PDF NUMBER 24)
QUESTION: Detect hierarchical depth of each employee in the org chart.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE employee_depth AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id, 1 AS depth
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id IS NULL

     UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id, ed.depth + 1
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employee_depth ed ON e.manager_id = ed.id
   )
   SELECT * FROM employee_depth;

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e1.name AS Employee1, e2.name AS
   Employee2, e1.department_id
   FROM employees e1
   JOIN employees e2 ON e1.department_id =
   e2.department_id AND e1.id < e2.id;

QUESTION ITEM 033 (PDF NUMBER 28)
QUESTION: Identify employees who haven't received a salary raise in more than a year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: salary_history
COLUMNS: history_id | employee_id | salary | raise_date | change_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 2 | 90000 | 2023-01-01 | 2023-01-01
  2 | 2 | 95000 | 2024-03-01 | 2024-03-01
  3 | 3 | 80000 | 2023-06-01 | 2023-06-01
  4 | 3 | 85000 | 2025-01-01 | 2025-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name
   FROM employees e
   JOIN salary_history sh ON e.id = sh.employee_id
   GROUP BY e.id, e.name
   HAVING MAX(sh.raise_date) < CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year';

QUESTION ITEM 034 (PDF NUMBER 29)
QUESTION: Write a query to rank salespeople by monthly sales, resetting the rank every month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT salesperson_id, sale_month, total_sales,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY sale_month
   ORDER BY total_sales DESC) AS monthly_rank
   FROM (
   SELECT salesperson_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS sale_month, SUM(amount) AS
   total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY salesperson_id, sale_month
   ) AS monthly_sales;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 035 (PDF NUMBER 30)
QUESTION: Calculate the percentage change in sales compared to the previous month for each

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
product.
   SELECT product_id, sale_month, total_sales,
   (total_sales - LAG(total_sales) OVER
   (PARTITION BY product_id ORDER BY
   sale_month)) * 100.0 /

   LAG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY sale_month) AS pct_change
   FROM (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS sale_month, SUM(amount) AS
   total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, sale_month
   ) monthly_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 037 (PDF NUMBER 32)
QUESTION: Retrieve the last 5 orders for each customer.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM (
   SELECT o.*, ROW_NUMBER() OVER
   (PARTITION BY customer_id ORDER BY order_date
   DESC) AS rn
   FROM orders o
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 5;

  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30

QUESTION ITEM 041 (PDF NUMBER 31)
QUESTION: Write a query to detect circular references in employee-manager hierarchy (cycles).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE mgr_path (id, manager_id, path)
   AS (
   SELECT id, manager_id, ARRAY[id]
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id IS NOT NULL
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.manager_id, path || e.id
   FROM employees e
   JOIN mgr_path mp ON e.manager_id = mp.id
   WHERE NOT e.id = ANY(path)
   )
   SELECT DISTINCT id
   FROM mgr_path
   WHERE id = ANY(path);

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 042 (PDF NUMBER 32)
QUESTION: Write a query to get the running total of sales per customer, ordered by sale date.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id, sale_date, amount,
   SUM(amount) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY sale_date) AS running_total
   FROM sales;

QUESTION ITEM 043 (PDF NUMBER 33)
QUESTION: Find the department-wise salary percentile (e.g., 90th percentile) using window functions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, salary,
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.9) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY salary) OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id) AS pct_90_salary
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 044 (PDF NUMBER 34)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salary is a prime number.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH primes AS (
   SELECT generate_series(2, (SELECT MAX(salary)
   FROM employees)) AS num
   EXCEPT
   SELECT num FROM (
   SELECT num, UNNEST(ARRAY(
   SELECT generate_series(2, FLOOR(SQRT(num)))
   AS divisor
   )) AS divisor
   WHERE num % divisor = 0
   ) AS composite
   )
   SELECT *
   FROM employees
   WHERE salary IN (SELECT num FROM primes);
   Note: This is a conceptual approach-some databases
   may not support this syntax fully.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 045 (PDF NUMBER 35)
QUESTION: Find employees who have worked for multiple departments over time.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

COLUMNS: employee_id | department_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 10 | 2020-06-01 | NULL
  5 | 20 | 2022-05-20 | NULL
  7 | 30 | 2021-09-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT employee_id
   FROM employee_department_history
   GROUP BY employee_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT department_id) > 1;

QUESTION ITEM 046 (PDF NUMBER 36)
QUESTION: Use window function to find the difference between current row's sales and previous row's

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
sales partitioned by product.
   SELECT product_id, sale_date, amount,
   amount - LAG(amount) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY sale_date) AS sales_diff
   FROM sales;

QUESTION ITEM 048 (PDF NUMBER 38)
QUESTION: Find average order value per month and product category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', order_date) AS
   order_month, category_id, AVG(order_value) AS
   avg_order_value
   FROM orders

     GROUP BY order_month, category_id;

QUESTION ITEM 049 (PDF NUMBER 39)
QUESTION: Write a query to create a running count of how many employees joined in each year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT join_year, COUNT(*) AS yearly_hires,
   SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (ORDER BY join_year)
   AS running_total_hires
   FROM (
   SELECT EXTRACT(YEAR FROM hire_date) AS
   join_year
   FROM employees
   ) sub
   GROUP BY join_year
   ORDER BY join_year;

QUESTION ITEM 050 (PDF NUMBER 40)
QUESTION: Write a query to find the second most recent order date per customer.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id, order_date
   FROM (
   SELECT customer_id, order_date,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY order_date DESC) AS rn
   FROM orders
   ) sub
   WHERE rn = 2;

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15

  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000

   FROM employees e

    LEFT JOIN sales s ON e.id = s.employee_id

  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 053 (PDF NUMBER 43)
QUESTION: Get employees with salary in the top 10% in their department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM (
   SELECT e.*, NTILE(10) OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id ORDER BY salary DESC) AS decile
   FROM employees e
   ) sub
   WHERE decile = 1;

COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500

QUESTION ITEM 055 (PDF NUMBER 45)
QUESTION: List all departments and their employee counts, including departments with zero

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
employees.
   SELECT d.department_id, d.department_name,
   COUNT(e.id) AS employee_count
   FROM departments d
   LEFT JOIN employees e ON d.department_id =
   e.department_id
   GROUP BY d.department_id, d.department_name;

   FROM table_name
   GROUP BY column1, column2

QUESTION ITEM 057 (PDF NUMBER 42)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to calculate factorial of a number (e.g., 5).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE factorial(n, fact) AS (
   SELECT 1, 1
   UNION ALL
   SELECT n + 1, fact * (n + 1)
   FROM factorial
   WHERE n < 5
   )
   SELECT fact FROM factorial WHERE n = 5;

QUESTION ITEM 058 (PDF NUMBER 43)
QUESTION: Write a query to calculate the cumulative percentage of total sales per product.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id, sale_amount,

    SUM(sale_amount) OVER (ORDER BY
   sale_amount DESC) * 100.0 / SUM(sale_amount)
   OVER () AS cumulative_pct
   FROM sales;

QUESTION ITEM 059 (PDF NUMBER 44)
QUESTION: Write a query to get employees who reported directly or indirectly to a given manager

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
(hierarchy traversal).
   WITH RECURSIVE reporting AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id = 101 -- replace 101 with
   manager's id
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id
   FROM employees e
   INNER JOIN reporting r ON e.manager_id = r.id
   )
   SELECT * FROM reporting;

QUESTION ITEM 060 (PDF NUMBER 45)
QUESTION: Find the average number of orders per customer and standard deviation.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT AVG(order_count) AS avg_orders,
   STDDEV(order_count) AS stddev_orders
   FROM (
   SELECT customer_id, COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id

     ) sub;

QUESTION ITEM 061 (PDF NUMBER 46)
QUESTION: Find gaps in date sequences for each customer (missing days).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH dates AS (
   SELECT customer_id, purchase_date,
   LAG(purchase_date) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY purchase_date) AS prev_date
   FROM sales
   )
   SELECT customer_id, prev_date + INTERVAL '1 day'
   AS missing_date
   FROM dates
   WHERE purchase_date > prev_date + INTERVAL '1
   day';

QUESTION ITEM 062 (PDF NUMBER 47)
QUESTION: Rank employees by salary within their department, and calculate percent rank.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT name, department_id, salary,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY department_id
   ORDER BY salary DESC) AS salary_rank,
   PERCENT_RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id ORDER BY salary DESC) AS
   salary_percent_rank
   FROM employees;

QUESTION ITEM 063 (PDF NUMBER 48)
QUESTION: Find products that have never been sold.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p.product_id, p.product_name
   FROM products p
   LEFT JOIN sales s ON p.product_id = s.product_id

     WHERE s.sale_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 064 (PDF NUMBER 49)
QUESTION: Write a query to find consecutive days where sales were above a threshold.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH flagged_sales AS (
   SELECT sale_date, amount,
   CASE WHEN amount > 1000 THEN 1 ELSE 0
   END AS flag
   FROM sales
   ),
   groups AS (
   SELECT sale_date, amount, flag,
   sale_date - INTERVAL ROW_NUMBER()
   OVER (ORDER BY sale_date) DAY AS grp
   FROM flagged_sales
   WHERE flag = 1
   )
   SELECT MIN(sale_date) AS start_date,
   MAX(sale_date) AS end_date, COUNT(*) AS
   consecutive_days
   FROM groups
   GROUP BY grp
   ORDER BY consecutive_days DESC;

  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount

   WHERE category_id = 10

QUESTION ITEM 069 (PDF NUMBER 54)
QUESTION: Find employees with no corresponding entries in the salary_history table.

TABLE: employees

  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12

QUESTION ITEM 071 (PDF NUMBER 51)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to list all ancestors (managers) of a given employee.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE ancestors AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id

     FROM employees
   WHERE id = 123 -- given employee id
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id
   FROM employees e
   JOIN ancestors a ON e.id = a.manager_id
   )
   SELECT * FROM ancestors WHERE id != 123;

QUESTION ITEM 072 (PDF NUMBER 52)
QUESTION: Calculate the median salary by department using window functions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DISTINCT department_id,
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.5) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY salary) OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id) AS median_salary
   FROM employees;

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000

QUESTION ITEM 074 (PDF NUMBER 54)
QUESTION: Find the percentage difference between each month's total sales and the previous month's

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
total sales.
   WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', sale_date) AS
   month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY month
   )
   SELECT month, total_sales,
   (total_sales - LAG(total_sales) OVER (ORDER
   BY month)) * 100.0 / LAG(total_sales) OVER
   (ORDER BY month) AS pct_change
   FROM monthly_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 075 (PDF NUMBER 55)
QUESTION: Write a query to find employees who have the longest tenure within their department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   SELECT *,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY department_id
   ORDER BY hire_date ASC) AS tenure_rank
   FROM employees
   )
   SELECT *
   FROM tenure
   WHERE tenure_rank = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 076 (PDF NUMBER 56)
QUESTION: Generate a report that shows sales and sales growth percentage compared to the same

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
month last year.
   WITH monthly_sales AS (

     SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', sale_date) AS
   month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY month
   )
   SELECT ms1.month, ms1.total_sales,
   ((ms1.total_sales - ms2.total_sales) * 100.0 /
   ms2.total_sales) AS growth_pct
   FROM monthly_sales ms1
   LEFT JOIN monthly_sales ms2 ON ms1.month =
   ms2.month + INTERVAL '1 year';

QUESTION ITEM 077 (PDF NUMBER 57)
QUESTION: Write a query to identify overlapping shifts for employees.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: shifts
COLUMNS: shift_id | employee_id | start_time | end_time
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 2 | 2025-01-01 09:00 | 2025-01-01 17:00
  2 | 2 | 2025-01-01 16:00 | 2025-01-01 22:00
  3 | 3 | 2025-01-02 09:00 | 2025-01-02 17:00

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT s1.employee_id, s1.shift_id AS shift1,
   s2.shift_id AS shift2
   FROM shifts s1
   JOIN shifts s2 ON s1.employee_id = s2.employee_id
   AND s1.shift_id <> s2.shift_id
   WHERE s1.start_time < s2.end_time AND s1.end_time
   > s2.start_time;

QUESTION ITEM 078 (PDF NUMBER 58)
QUESTION: Calculate the total revenue for each customer, and rank them from highest to lowest

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   SELECT customer_id, SUM(amount) AS
   total_revenue,
   RANK() OVER (ORDER BY SUM(amount)
   DESC) AS revenue_rank
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY customer_id;

QUESTION ITEM 080 (PDF NUMBER 60)
QUESTION: Write a query to find the top 3 products with the highest total sales amount each month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_product_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   ranked_sales AS (
   SELECT *, RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY month
   ORDER BY total_sales DESC) AS sales_rank
   FROM monthly_product_sales
   )
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales
   FROM ranked_sales
   WHERE sales_rank <= 3
   ORDER BY month, sales_rank;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 081 (PDF NUMBER 61)
QUESTION: Find the customers who placed orders only in the last 30 days.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '30 days'
   AND customer_id NOT IN (
   SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date < CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '30 days'
   );

  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000

   FROM products p
   LEFT JOIN orders o ON p.product_id = o.product_id
   WHERE o.order_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 084 (PDF NUMBER 64)
QUESTION: Calculate the total sales amount and number of orders per customer in the last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id, COUNT(*) AS total_orders,
   SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   WHERE sale_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year'
   GROUP BY customer_id;

QUESTION ITEM 085 (PDF NUMBER 65)
QUESTION: List the top 5 highest-paid employees per department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM (
   SELECT e.*, ROW_NUMBER() OVER
   (PARTITION BY department_id ORDER BY salary
   DESC) AS rn
   FROM employees e
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 5;

QUESTION ITEM 086 (PDF NUMBER 61)
QUESTION: Write a query to identify "gaps and islands" in attendance records (consecutive dates

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: attendance
COLUMNS: employee_id | attendance_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  2 | 2025-01-01
  2 | 2025-01-02
  2 | 2025-01-03
  3 | 2025-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
present).
   WITH attendance_groups AS (
   SELECT employee_id, attendance_date,
   attendance_date - INTERVAL ROW_NUMBER()
   OVER (PARTITION BY employee_id ORDER BY
   attendance_date) DAY AS grp
   FROM attendance
   )
   SELECT employee_id, MIN(attendance_date) AS
   start_date, MAX(attendance_date) AS end_date,
   COUNT(*) AS consecutive_days

    FROM attendance_groups
   GROUP BY employee_id, grp
   ORDER BY employee_id, start_date;

QUESTION ITEM 087 (PDF NUMBER 62)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to list all descendants of a manager in an organizational hierarchy.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE descendants AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id = 100 -- starting manager id
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id
   FROM employees e
   INNER JOIN descendants d ON e.manager_id = d.id
   )
   SELECT * FROM descendants;

QUESTION ITEM 088 (PDF NUMBER 63)
QUESTION: Calculate a 3-month moving average of monthly sales per product.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   )
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   AVG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month ROWS BETWEEN 2

    PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) AS
   moving_avg
   FROM monthly_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 089 (PDF NUMBER 64)
QUESTION: Write a query to find employees who have the same hire date as their managers.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name AS employee_name, m.name AS
   manager_name, e.hire_date
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE e.hire_date = m.hire_date;

QUESTION ITEM 090 (PDF NUMBER 65)
QUESTION: Write a query to find products with increasing sales over the last 3 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   ranked_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month DESC) AS rn
   FROM monthly_sales
   )
   SELECT ms1.product_id
   FROM ranked_sales ms1
   JOIN ranked_sales ms2 ON ms1.product_id =
   ms2.product_id AND ms1.rn = 1 AND ms2.rn = 2

    JOIN ranked_sales ms3 ON ms1.product_id =
   ms3.product_id AND ms3.rn = 3
   WHERE ms3.total_sales < ms2.total_sales AND
   ms2.total_sales < ms1.total_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 091 (PDF NUMBER 66)
QUESTION: Write a query to get the nth highest salary per department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, salary
   FROM (
   SELECT department_id, salary, ROW_NUMBER()
   OVER (PARTITION BY department_id ORDER BY
   salary DESC) AS rn
   FROM employees
   ) sub
   WHERE rn = N;
   (Replace N with the desired rank.)

  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30

   --68. Write a query to calculate the difference in days
   between each employee's hire date and their manager's
   hire date.

    SELECT e.name AS employee, m.name AS manager,
   DATEDIFF(day, m.hire_date, e.hire_date) AS
   days_difference
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id;
   (Syntax of DATEDIFF varies by SQL dialect)

QUESTION ITEM 093 (PDF NUMBER 69)
QUESTION: Write a query to find the department with the highest average years of experience.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, AVG(EXTRACT(year FROM
   CURRENT_DATE - hire_date)) AS
   avg_experience_years
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   ORDER BY avg_experience_years DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 094 (PDF NUMBER 70)
QUESTION: Identify employees who had overlapping project assignments.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p1.employee_id, p1.project_id AS project1,
   p2.project_id AS project2
   FROM project_assignments p1
   JOIN project_assignments p2 ON p1.employee_id =
   p2.employee_id AND p1.project_id <> p2.project_id
   WHERE p1.start_date < p2.end_date AND p1.end_date
   > p2.start_date;

QUESTION ITEM 095 (PDF NUMBER 71)
QUESTION: Find customers who made purchases in every month of the current year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH months AS (

   SELECT generate_series(1, 12) AS month
   ),
   customer_months AS (
   SELECT customer_id, EXTRACT(MONTH FROM
   purchase_date) AS month
   FROM sales
   WHERE EXTRACT(YEAR FROM purchase_date) =
   EXTRACT(YEAR FROM CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY customer_id, EXTRACT(MONTH
   FROM purchase_date)
   )
   SELECT customer_id
   FROM customer_months
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT month) = 12;

SAMPLE ROWS:

  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20

QUESTION ITEM 097 (PDF NUMBER 73)
QUESTION: Get the product with the highest sales for each category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH category_sales AS (
   SELECT category_id, product_id, SUM(amount) AS
   total_sales,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY category_id
   ORDER BY SUM(amount) DESC) AS sales_rank
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY category_id, product_id
   )
   SELECT category_id, product_id, total_sales
   FROM category_sales
   WHERE sales_rank = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 098 (PDF NUMBER 74)
QUESTION: Find customers who haven't ordered in the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id
   FROM customers c
   LEFT JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id =
   o.customer_id
   GROUP BY c.customer_id
   HAVING MAX(o.order_date) < CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 months' OR MAX(o.order_date) IS
   NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 099 (PDF NUMBER 75)
QUESTION: Find the maximum salary gap between any two employees within the same department.

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01

QUESTION ITEM 100 (PDF NUMBER 71)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to compute the total budget under each manager (including

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
subordinates).
   WITH RECURSIVE manager_budget AS (
   SELECT id, manager_id, budget
   FROM departments
   UNION ALL
   SELECT d.id, d.manager_id, mb.budget
   FROM departments d
   JOIN manager_budget mb ON d.manager_id = mb.id
   )
   SELECT manager_id, SUM(budget) AS total_budget
   FROM manager_budget
   GROUP BY manager_id;

QUESTION ITEM 101 (PDF NUMBER 72)
QUESTION: Write a query to detect gaps in a sequence of invoice numbers.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: invoices
COLUMNS: invoice_number | invoice_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 2025-01-01
  1002 | 2025-01-02
  1004 | 2025-01-04

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH numbered_invoices AS (
   SELECT invoice_number, ROW_NUMBER() OVER
   (ORDER BY invoice_number) AS rn
   FROM invoices
   )
   SELECT invoice_number + 1 AS missing_invoice
   FROM numbered_invoices ni
   WHERE (invoice_number + 1) <> (
   SELECT invoice_number FROM numbered_invoices

   WHERE rn = ni.rn + 1

     );

QUESTION ITEM 102 (PDF NUMBER 73)
QUESTION: Calculate the rank of employees by salary within their department but restart rank

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
numbering every 10 employees.
   WITH ranked_employees AS (
   SELECT e.*,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   department_id ORDER BY salary DESC) AS rn
   FROM employees e
   )
   SELECT *, ((rn - 1) / 10) + 1 AS rank_group
   FROM ranked_employees;
