QUESTION ITEM 103 (PDF NUMBER 74)
QUESTION: Find the moving median of daily sales over the last 7 days for each product.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH daily_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, sale_date, SUM(amount) AS
   total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, sale_date
   )
   SELECT product_id, sale_date,
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.5) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY total_sales)
   OVER (PARTITION BY product_id ORDER BY
   sale_date ROWS BETWEEN 6 PRECEDING AND
   CURRENT ROW) AS moving_median
   FROM daily_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 104 (PDF NUMBER 75)
QUESTION: Find customers who purchased both product A and product B.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000

  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id
   FROM sales
   WHERE product_id IN ('A', 'B')
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT product_id) = 2;

QUESTION ITEM 105 (PDF NUMBER 76)
QUESTION: Write a query to generate a calendar table with all dates for the current year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT generate_series(
   DATE_TRUNC('year', CURRENT_DATE),
   DATE_TRUNC('year', CURRENT_DATE) +
   INTERVAL '1 year' - INTERVAL '1 day',
   INTERVAL '1 day'
   ) AS calendar_date;

QUESTION ITEM 107 (PDF NUMBER 78)
QUESTION: Calculate the percentage contribution of each product's sales to the total sales per month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS product_sales
   FROM sales

     GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   total_monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT month, SUM(product_sales) AS total_sales
   FROM monthly_sales
   GROUP BY month
   )
   SELECT ms.product_id, ms.month, ms.product_sales,
   (ms.product_sales * 100.0) / tms.total_sales AS
   pct_contribution
   FROM monthly_sales ms
   JOIN total_monthly_sales tms ON ms.month =
   tms.month;

QUESTION ITEM 108 (PDF NUMBER 79)
QUESTION: Write a query to pivot monthly sales data for each product into columns.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id,
   SUM(CASE WHEN EXTRACT(MONTH FROM
   sale_date) = 1 THEN amount ELSE 0 END) AS Jan,
   SUM(CASE WHEN EXTRACT(MONTH FROM
   sale_date) = 2 THEN amount ELSE 0 END) AS Feb,

   -- Repeat for other months
   SUM(CASE WHEN EXTRACT(MONTH FROM
   sale_date) = 12 THEN amount ELSE 0 END) AS Dec
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id;

QUESTION ITEM 109 (PDF NUMBER 80)
QUESTION: Find the 3 most recent orders per customer including order details.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:

SAMPLE ROWS:

  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

    FROM (
   SELECT o.*, ROW_NUMBER() OVER
   (PARTITION BY customer_id ORDER BY order_date
   DESC) AS rn
   FROM orders o
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 3;

  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 111 (PDF NUMBER 82)
QUESTION: List customers who placed orders in January but not in February.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH jan_orders AS (
   SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders
   WHERE EXTRACT(MONTH FROM order_date) = 1
   ),
   feb_orders AS (
   SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders
   WHERE EXTRACT(MONTH FROM order_date) = 2
   )
   SELECT customer_id

    FROM jan_orders
   WHERE customer_id NOT IN (SELECT customer_id
   FROM feb_orders);

QUESTION ITEM 112 (PDF NUMBER 83)
QUESTION: Find products that have seen a price increase in the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: product_prices
COLUMNS: product_id | price | effective_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | 75000 | 2024-01-01
  P1 | 80000 | 2025-01-01
  P2 | 1500 | 2024-02-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH price_changes AS (
   SELECT product_id, price, effective_date,
   LAG(price) OVER (PARTITION BY product_id
   ORDER BY effective_date) AS prev_price
   FROM product_prices
   WHERE effective_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 months'
   )
   SELECT DISTINCT product_id
   FROM price_changes
   WHERE price > prev_price;

QUESTION ITEM 113 (PDF NUMBER 84)
QUESTION: Find the department(s) with the second highest average salary.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_salaries AS (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   ),
   ranked_salaries AS (
   SELECT department_id, avg_salary,
   DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY avg_salary
   DESC) AS rnk
   FROM avg_salaries

    )
   SELECT department_id, avg_salary
   FROM ranked_salaries
   WHERE rnk = 2;

QUESTION ITEM 114 (PDF NUMBER 85)
QUESTION: Find employees who joined in the same month and year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e1.id AS emp1_id, e2.id AS emp2_id,
   e1.hire_date
   FROM employees e1
   JOIN employees e2 ON e1.id <> e2.id
   AND EXTRACT(MONTH FROM e1.hire_date) =
   EXTRACT(MONTH FROM e2.hire_date)
   AND EXTRACT(YEAR FROM e1.hire_date) =
   EXTRACT(YEAR FROM e2.hire_date);

QUESTION ITEM 115 (PDF NUMBER 81)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to find all employees and their level of reporting (distance from

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
CEO).
   WITH RECURSIVE hierarchy AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id, 1 AS level
   FROM employees
   WHERE manager_id IS NULL -- CEO level
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id, h.level + 1
   FROM employees e
   JOIN hierarchy h ON e.manager_id = h.id
   )

    SELECT * FROM hierarchy ORDER BY level,
   manager_id;

QUESTION ITEM 116 (PDF NUMBER 82)
QUESTION: Find the second highest salary per department without using window functions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, MAX(salary) AS
   second_highest_salary
   FROM employees e1
   WHERE salary < (
   SELECT MAX(salary)
   FROM employees e2
   WHERE e2.department_id = e1.department_id
   )
   GROUP BY department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 117 (PDF NUMBER 83)
QUESTION: Calculate the percentage change in sales for each product comparing current month to

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
previous month.
   WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   )
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   (total_sales - LAG(total_sales) OVER
   (PARTITION BY product_id ORDER BY month)) *
   100.0
   / LAG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month) AS pct_change
   FROM monthly_sales;

   FROM table_name
   WHERE cnt > 1;
   (Replace col1, col2, col3, ... with actual column names)

QUESTION ITEM 119 (PDF NUMBER 85)
QUESTION: Write a query to unpivot quarterly sales data into rows.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id, 'Q1' AS quarter, Q1_sales AS
   sales FROM sales_data
   UNION ALL
   SELECT product_id, 'Q2', Q2_sales FROM sales_data
   UNION ALL
   SELECT product_id, 'Q3', Q3_sales FROM sales_data
   UNION ALL
   SELECT product_id, 'Q4', Q4_sales FROM sales_data;

QUESTION ITEM 121 (PDF NUMBER 87)
QUESTION: Write a query to find customers with the highest purchase amount per year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH yearly_sales AS (

   SELECT customer_id, EXTRACT(YEAR FROM
   sale_date) AS year, SUM(amount) AS total_amount
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY customer_id, year
   ),
   ranked_sales AS (
   SELECT *, RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY year
   ORDER BY total_amount DESC) AS rnk
   FROM yearly_sales
   )
   SELECT customer_id, year, total_amount
   FROM ranked_sales
   WHERE rnk = 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 122 (PDF NUMBER 88)
QUESTION: Write a query to list all employees who have a salary equal to the average salary of their

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
department.
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   ) d ON e.department_id = d.department_id AND
   e.salary = d.avg_salary;

QUESTION ITEM 126 (PDF NUMBER 92)
QUESTION: Find the total sales per customer including those with zero sales.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT c.customer_id, COALESCE(SUM(s.amount),
   0) AS total_sales
   FROM customers c
   LEFT JOIN sales s ON c.customer_id = s.customer_id

     GROUP BY c.customer_id;

QUESTION ITEM 127 (PDF NUMBER 93)

TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12

   FROM employees

   d.department_id AND e.salary = d.max_salary;

QUESTION ITEM 128 (PDF NUMBER 94)
QUESTION: Find customers with no orders in the last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id
   FROM customers c
   LEFT JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id =
   o.customer_id AND o.order_date >=
   CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '1 year'
   WHERE o.order_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 129 (PDF NUMBER 95)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salary is within 10% of the highest salary in their department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH dept_max AS (
   SELECT department_id, MAX(salary) AS max_salary
   FROM employees

   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT e.*

    FROM employees e
   JOIN dept_max d ON e.department_id =
   d.department_id
   WHERE e.salary >= 0.9 * d.max_salary;

QUESTION ITEM 130 (PDF NUMBER 96)
QUESTION: Find the running total of sales by date.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT sale_date, SUM(amount) OVER (ORDER BY
   sale_date ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED
   PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) AS
   running_total
   FROM sales
   ORDER BY sale_date;

QUESTION ITEM 132 (PDF NUMBER 98)
QUESTION: Get the last 3 orders placed by each customer.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT *
   FROM (
   SELECT o.*, ROW_NUMBER() OVER
   (PARTITION BY customer_id ORDER BY order_date
   DESC) AS rn
   FROM orders o
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 3;

QUESTION ITEM 136 (PDF NUMBER 102)
QUESTION: Get the total number of orders per day, including days with zero orders.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH dates AS (
   SELECT generate_series(MIN(order_date),
   MAX(order_date), INTERVAL '1 day') AS day
   FROM orders
   )
   SELECT d.day, COUNT(o.order_id) AS order_count

    FROM dates d
   LEFT JOIN orders o ON d.day = o.order_date
   GROUP BY d.day
   ORDER BY d.day;

QUESTION ITEM 138 (PDF NUMBER 104)
QUESTION: Write a query to find gaps in employee IDs.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH numbered AS (
   SELECT id, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY
   id) AS rn

   FROM employees
   )
   SELECT rn + 1 AS missing_id
   FROM numbered
   WHERE id <> rn;

QUESTION ITEM 139 (PDF NUMBER 105)
QUESTION: Find employees who were hired before their managers.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name AS employee, m.name AS manager,
   e.hire_date, m.hire_date AS manager_hire_date
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE e.hire_date < m.hire_date;

QUESTION ITEM 140 (PDF NUMBER 106)

  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 141 (PDF NUMBER 107)
QUESTION: Find employees with the highest number of dependents.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT employee_id, COUNT(*) AS
   dependent_count
   FROM dependents
   GROUP BY employee_id
   ORDER BY dependent_count DESC
   LIMIT 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------


========================================================================
QUESTION ITEM 142 (PDF NUMBER 108)
QUESTION: Find customers with the longest gap between two consecutive orders.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH ordered_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, order_date,
   LAG(order_date) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY order_date) AS
   prev_order_date
   FROM orders
   ),
   gaps AS (
   SELECT customer_id, order_date - prev_order_date
   AS gap_days

     FROM ordered_orders
   WHERE prev_order_date IS NOT NULL
   )
   SELECT customer_id, MAX(gap_days) AS
   longest_gap
   FROM gaps
   GROUP BY customer_id
   ORDER BY longest_gap DESC
   LIMIT 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 143 (PDF NUMBER 109)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered all products in a category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id
   FROM sales
   WHERE product_id IN (SELECT product_id FROM
   products WHERE category_id = 1)
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT product_id) = (SELECT
   COUNT(*) FROM products WHERE category_id = 1);

QUESTION ITEM 147 (PDF NUMBER 113)
QUESTION: List products with sales above the average sales amount.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_sales AS (
   SELECT AVG(amount) AS avg_amount
   FROM sales
   )
   SELECT product_id, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING SUM(amount) > (SELECT avg_amount
   FROM avg_sales);

  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 154 (PDF NUMBER 120)
QUESTION: Calculate total sales by product category.

COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p.category_id, SUM(s.amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id
   GROUP BY p.category_id;

QUESTION ITEM 159 (PDF NUMBER 125)
QUESTION: Find products with sales only in the current month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING MAX(sale_date) >=
   DATE_TRUNC('month', CURRENT_DATE)
   AND MIN(sale_date) >= DATE_TRUNC('month',
   CURRENT_DATE);

QUESTION ITEM 160 (PDF NUMBER 126)
QUESTION: Find employees with consecutive workdays.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: attendance
COLUMNS: employee_id | attendance_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  2 | 2025-01-01
  2 | 2025-01-02
  2 | 2025-01-03
  3 | 2025-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH attendance AS (
   SELECT employee_id, work_date,
   work_date - INTERVAL '1 day' *
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   employee_id ORDER BY work_date) AS grp
   FROM work_log
   )
   SELECT employee_id, COUNT(*) AS
   consecutive_days
   FROM attendance
   GROUP BY employee_id, grp
   HAVING COUNT(*) > 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 161 (PDF NUMBER 127)
QUESTION: Find the average number of orders per customer.

COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT AVG(order_count) AS
   avg_orders_per_customer
   FROM (

    SELECT customer_id, COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id
   ) sub;

QUESTION: Find employees who have worked on more than 5 projects.

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   AS project_count
   FROM project_assignments
   GROUP BY employee_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT project_id) > 5;

QUESTION ITEM 168 (PDF NUMBER 134)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered products only from one category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:

COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East

  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15

  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18

QUESTION ITEM 170 (PDF NUMBER 136)
QUESTION: Find products with sales increasing every month for the last 3 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   WHERE sale_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '3 months'
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   )
   SELECT product_id
   FROM monthly_sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING COUNT(*) = 3
   AND MIN(total_sales) < MAX(total_sales)
   AND total_sales[1] < total_sales[2] AND
   total_sales[2] < total_sales[3]; -- depends on DB
   support
   Note: Exact syntax varies per RDBMS; may need
   window functions.

QUESTION ITEM 171 (PDF NUMBER 137)
QUESTION: Write a recursive query to get all descendants of a manager.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH RECURSIVE subordinates AS (
   SELECT id, name, manager_id
   FROM employees
   WHERE id = :manager_id
   UNION ALL
   SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id
   FROM employees e
   JOIN subordinates s ON e.manager_id = s.id
   )
   SELECT * FROM subordinates WHERE id !=
   :manager_id;

QUESTION ITEM 172 (PDF NUMBER 138)
QUESTION: Find the department with the highest variance in salaries.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, VAR_SAMP(salary) AS
   salary_variance
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   ORDER BY salary_variance DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 173 (PDF NUMBER 139)
QUESTION: Calculate the difference between each order amount and the previous order amount per

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
customer.
   SELECT customer_id, order_date, amount,

    amount - LAG(amount) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY order_date) AS diff
   FROM orders;

SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000

QUESTION ITEM 176 (PDF NUMBER 142)
QUESTION: Find the month with the highest sales in the current year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', sale_date) AS
   month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   WHERE EXTRACT(YEAR FROM sale_date) =
   EXTRACT(YEAR FROM CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY month
   ORDER BY total_sales DESC
   LIMIT 1;

COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 179 (PDF NUMBER 145)
QUESTION: Get the average salary of employees hired each year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01

QUESTION ITEM 180 (PDF NUMBER 146)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salaries are between the 25th and 75th percentile.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH percentiles AS (
   SELECT
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.25) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY salary) AS p25,
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.75) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY salary) AS p75

     FROM employees
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e, percentiles p
   WHERE e.salary BETWEEN p.p25 AND p.p75;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 181 (PDF NUMBER 147)
QUESTION: Find employees with salaries higher than their department average.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   ) d ON e.department_id = d.department_id
   WHERE e.salary > d.avg_salary;

   --148. Find the difference between each row's value and
   the previous row's value in sales.
   SELECT sale_date, amount,
   amount - LAG(amount) OVER (ORDER BY
   sale_date) AS diff
   FROM sales;

QUESTION ITEM 183 (PDF NUMBER 150)

  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.department_id, COUNT(*) AS
   promotion_count

   FROM promotions p
   JOIN employees e ON p.employee_id = e.id
   GROUP BY e.department_id

   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 184 (PDF NUMBER 151)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered products from at least 3 different categories.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500

  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT p.category_id) >= 3;

QUESTION ITEM 185 (PDF NUMBER 152)
QUESTION: Find the average gap (in days) between orders per customer.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH ordered_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, order_date,
   LAG(order_date) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY order_date) AS
   prev_order_date
   FROM orders
   ),

     gaps AS (
   SELECT customer_id, order_date - prev_order_date
   AS gap
   FROM ordered_orders
   WHERE prev_order_date IS NOT NULL
   )
   SELECT customer_id, AVG(gap) AS avg_gap_days
   FROM gaps
   GROUP BY customer_id;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 187 (PDF NUMBER 154)
QUESTION: Calculate total revenue and number of orders per country.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT c.country, COUNT(o.order_id) AS
   order_count, SUM(o.amount) AS total_revenue
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id = o.customer_id
   GROUP BY c.country;

QUESTION ITEM 189 (PDF NUMBER 156)
QUESTION: Find the top 3 products by quantity sold in each category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT category_id, product_id, total_quantity
   FROM (
   SELECT p.category_id, s.product_id,
   SUM(s.quantity) AS total_quantity,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   p.category_id ORDER BY SUM(s.quantity) DESC) AS
   rn
   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id
   GROUP BY p.category_id, s.product_id
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 3;

QUESTION: Find the difference in days between the first and last order for each customer.

QUESTION ITEM 191 (PDF NUMBER 158)
QUESTION: Find customers who have increased their order volume every month for the last 3 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',

   order_date) AS month, COUNT(*) AS orders_count
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '3 months'
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   )
   SELECT customer_id
   FROM monthly_orders
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(*) = 3
   AND MIN(orders_count) < MAX(orders_count); -Needs detailed window check for strict increase

QUESTION ITEM 192 (PDF NUMBER 159)
QUESTION: Find employees who have the same salary as the average salary in their job title.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   FROM employees e

QUESTION ITEM 193 (PDF NUMBER 160)
QUESTION: Write a query to calculate the difference in salary between employees and their managers.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.name, m.name AS manager_name, e.salary,
   m.salary AS manager_salary, e.salary - m.salary AS
   salary_diff
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id;

  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01

QUESTION ITEM 195 (PDF NUMBER 162)
QUESTION: Find the employee with the maximum salary in each department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH dept_max AS (
   SELECT department_id, MAX(salary) AS max_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN dept_max d ON e.department_id =
   d.department_id AND e.salary = d.max_salary;

QUESTION ITEM 196 (PDF NUMBER 163)
QUESTION: Find customers with orders on every day in the last week.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH days AS (

     SELECT generate_series(CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 days', CURRENT_DATE, INTERVAL
   '1 day') AS day
   )
   SELECT customer_id
   FROM orders o
   JOIN days d ON o.order_date = d.day
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT o.order_date) = 7;

QUESTION ITEM 197 (PDF NUMBER 164)
QUESTION: Find the product that has been sold in the highest quantity in a single order.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id, MAX(quantity) AS
   max_quantity_in_order
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   ORDER BY max_quantity_in_order DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION: Find customers with sales in at least 3 different years.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY customer_id

   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT EXTRACT(YEAR
   FROM sale_date)) >= 3;

QUESTION ITEM 200 (PDF NUMBER 167)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salary is above the company's average but below their

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
department's average.
   WITH company_avg AS (SELECT AVG(salary) AS
   avg_salary FROM employees),
   dept_avg AS (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS
   avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e, company_avg ca
   JOIN dept_avg da ON e.department_id =
   da.department_id
   WHERE e.salary > ca.avg_salary AND e.salary <
   da.avg_salary;

QUESTION ITEM 201 (PDF NUMBER 168)
QUESTION: Find the average order amount per customer per year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:

COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used

  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

QUESTION ITEM 204 (PDF NUMBER 171)
QUESTION: Find customers who made orders totaling more than the average order amount.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_order AS (
   SELECT AVG(amount) AS avg_amount FROM
   orders
   )
   SELECT customer_id, SUM(amount) AS total_amount
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING SUM(amount) > (SELECT avg_amount
   FROM avg_order);

QUESTION ITEM 206 (PDF NUMBER 173)
QUESTION: Find the month with the lowest sales in the past year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', sale_date) AS
   month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   WHERE sale_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year'
   GROUP BY month
   ORDER BY total_sales
   LIMIT 1;

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30

  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   month, COUNT(*) AS hires
   FROM employees
   WHERE hire_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year'

QUESTION ITEM 210 (PDF NUMBER 177)
QUESTION: Get the total sales amount for each product category including categories with zero sales.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT c.category_id, COALESCE(SUM(s.amount),
   0) AS total_sales
   FROM categories c
   LEFT JOIN products p ON c.category_id =
   p.category_id
   LEFT JOIN sales s ON p.product_id = s.product_id
   GROUP BY c.category_id;

  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01

QUESTION ITEM 214 (PDF NUMBER 181)
QUESTION: Find products whose sales have doubled compared to the previous month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   sales_comparison AS (
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   LAG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_month_sales
   FROM monthly_sales
   )
   SELECT product_id, month
   FROM sales_comparison

    WHERE prev_month_sales IS NOT NULL AND
   total_sales >= 2 * prev_month_sales;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT employee_id, COUNT(DISTINCT project_id)
   AS project_count
   FROM project_assignments
   WHERE assignment_date BETWEEN '2023-01-01'
   AND '2023-12-31'
   GROUP BY employee_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT project_id) > 3;

QUESTION ITEM 217 (PDF NUMBER 184)
QUESTION: Find the average salary increase percentage per department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: promotions
COLUMNS: promotion_id | employee_id | promotion_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 2 | 2024-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.department_id, AVG((e.salary p.old_salary) / p.old_salary * 100) AS avg_increase_pct
   FROM employees e
   JOIN promotions p ON e.id = p.employee_id
   GROUP BY e.department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 219 (PDF NUMBER 186)
QUESTION: Find products ordered by all customers.

COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT customer_id) =
   (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM customers);

QUESTION ITEM 220 (PDF NUMBER 187)
QUESTION: Find customers with orders totaling more than $5000 in the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id, SUM(amount) AS total_amount
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 months'
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING SUM(amount) > 5000;

QUESTION ITEM 222 (PDF NUMBER 189)
QUESTION: Find customers who purchased a product but never reordered it.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH order_counts AS (
   SELECT customer_id, product_id, COUNT(*) AS
   order_count
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY customer_id, product_id
   )
   SELECT customer_id, product_id
   FROM order_counts
   WHERE order_count = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 225 (PDF NUMBER 192)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered the most products in 2023.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT customer_id, SUM(quantity) AS
   total_quantity

    FROM sales
   WHERE EXTRACT(YEAR FROM sale_date) = 2023
   GROUP BY customer_id
   ORDER BY total_quantity DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 227 (PDF NUMBER 194)
QUESTION: Find employees with overlapping project assignments.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT pa1.employee_id, pa1.project_id,
   pa2.project_id AS overlapping_project

   FROM project_assignments pa1
   JOIN project_assignments pa2 ON pa1.employee_id =
   pa2.employee_id AND pa1.project_id <>
   pa2.project_id
   WHERE pa1.start_date <= pa2.end_date AND
   pa1.end_date >= pa2.start_date;

QUESTION ITEM 228 (PDF NUMBER 195)
QUESTION: Find the total number of unique customers per product category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p.category_id, COUNT(DISTINCT
   s.customer_id) AS unique_customers
   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id

     GROUP BY p.category_id;

QUESTION ITEM 229 (PDF NUMBER 196)
QUESTION: Find customers whose orders increased by at least 20% compared to the previous month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   order_date) AS month, COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   ),
   orders_comparison AS (
   SELECT customer_id, month, order_count,
   LAG(order_count) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_order_count
   FROM monthly_orders
   )
   SELECT customer_id, month
   FROM orders_comparison
   WHERE prev_order_count IS NOT NULL AND
   order_count >= 1.2 * prev_order_count;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 230 (PDF NUMBER 197)
QUESTION: Find employees with no projects assigned in the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   LEFT JOIN project_assignments pa ON e.id =
   pa.employee_id AND pa.start_date >=
   CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '6 months'
   WHERE pa.project_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 234 (PDF NUMBER 201)
QUESTION: Find employees who have worked on every project in their department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.id, e.name
   FROM employees e
   JOIN projects p ON e.department_id = p.department_id
   LEFT JOIN project_assignments pa ON e.id =
   pa.employee_id AND p.project_id = pa.project_id
   GROUP BY e.id, e.name, p.department_id

    HAVING COUNT(p.project_id) =
   COUNT(pa.project_id);

QUESTION ITEM 235 (PDF NUMBER 202)
QUESTION: Find the average order amount excluding the top 5% largest orders.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH ordered_orders AS (
   SELECT amount,
   NTILE(100) OVER (ORDER BY amount DESC)
   AS percentile
   FROM orders

   )
   SELECT AVG(amount)
   FROM ordered_orders
   WHERE percentile > 5;
