QUESTION ITEM 236 (PDF NUMBER 203)
QUESTION: Find the top 3 employees with the highest salary increase over last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH salary_last_year AS (
   SELECT employee_id, salary AS last_year_salary
   FROM salaries
   WHERE year = EXTRACT(YEAR FROM
   CURRENT_DATE) - 1
   ),
   salary_this_year AS (
   SELECT employee_id, salary AS this_year_salary
   FROM salaries
   WHERE year = EXTRACT(YEAR FROM
   CURRENT_DATE)
   )

    SELECT t.employee_id, t.this_year_salary l.last_year_salary AS salary_increase
   FROM salary_this_year t
   JOIN salary_last_year l ON t.employee_id =
   l.employee_id
   ORDER BY salary_increase DESC
   LIMIT 3;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 237 (PDF NUMBER 204)
QUESTION: Find employees with the longest consecutive workdays.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: attendance
COLUMNS: employee_id | attendance_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  2 | 2025-01-01
  2 | 2025-01-02
  2 | 2025-01-03
  3 | 2025-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH workdays AS (
   SELECT employee_id, work_date,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   employee_id ORDER BY work_date) ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   employee_id ORDER BY work_date) AS grp
   FROM attendance
   ),
   consecutive_days AS (
   SELECT employee_id, COUNT(*) AS
   consecutive_days
   FROM workdays
   GROUP BY employee_id, grp
   )
   SELECT employee_id, MAX(consecutive_days) AS
   max_consecutive_days
   FROM consecutive_days
   GROUP BY employee_id;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 239 (PDF NUMBER 206)
QUESTION: Find the average salary of employees hired each month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT EXTRACT(YEAR FROM hire_date) AS
   year, EXTRACT(MONTH FROM hire_date) AS
   month, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY year, month
   ORDER BY year, month;

   --207. Find the first 5 orders after a customer's
   registration date.
   SELECT order_id, customer_id, order_date
   FROM (
   SELECT order_id, customer_id, order_date,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY order_date) AS rn
   FROM orders
   JOIN customers c ON orders.customer_id =
   c.customer_id
   WHERE order_date >= c.registration_date

    ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 5;

QUESTION ITEM 240 (PDF NUMBER 208)
QUESTION: Find customers who placed orders every month for the last 6 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH months AS (
   SELECT generate_series(
   DATE_TRUNC('month', CURRENT_DATE) INTERVAL '5 months',
   DATE_TRUNC('month', CURRENT_DATE),

   INTERVAL '1 month'
   ) AS month
   ),
   customer_months AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   order_date) AS month
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '6 months'
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   )
   SELECT customer_id
   FROM customer_months cm
   JOIN months m ON cm.month = m.month
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT cm.month) = 6;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 241 (PDF NUMBER 209)
QUESTION: Calculate the moving average of sales over the last 3 days.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT sale_date, product_id, amount,
   AVG(amount) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY sale_date ROWS BETWEEN 2
   PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) AS
   moving_avg_3_days
   FROM sales;

QUESTION ITEM 244 (PDF NUMBER 212)
QUESTION: Find the total sales for each product including products with zero sales.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p.product_id, COALESCE(SUM(s.amount),
   0) AS total_sales
   FROM products p
   LEFT JOIN sales s ON p.product_id = s.product_id

     GROUP BY p.product_id;

QUESTION ITEM 245 (PDF NUMBER 213)
QUESTION: List the top 5 employees by number of projects in each department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, employee_id, project_count
   FROM (
   SELECT e.department_id, pa.employee_id,
   COUNT(DISTINCT pa.project_id) AS project_count,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   e.department_id ORDER BY COUNT(DISTINCT
   pa.project_id) DESC) AS rn
   FROM project_assignments pa
   JOIN employees e ON pa.employee_id = e.id
   GROUP BY e.department_id, pa.employee_id
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 5;

QUESTION ITEM 246 (PDF NUMBER 214)
QUESTION: Find the day with the largest difference between maximum and minimum temperature.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT weather_date, MAX(temperature) MIN(temperature) AS temp_diff
   FROM weather_data
   GROUP BY weather_date
   ORDER BY temp_diff DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 250 (PDF NUMBER 218)
QUESTION: Find the percentage of employees in each department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH total_employees AS (SELECT COUNT(*) AS
   total FROM employees)
   SELECT department_id, COUNT(*) * 100.0 /
   (SELECT total FROM total_employees) AS percentage

    FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id;

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id,
   PERCENTILE_CONT(0.5) WITHIN GROUP
   (ORDER BY salary) AS median_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 252 (PDF NUMBER 220)
QUESTION: Find the employee who worked the most hours in a project.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:

COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   FROM project_assignments

   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 254 (PDF NUMBER 222)
QUESTION: Find the second most expensive product per category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500

  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

   FROM (

   FROM products
   ) sub
   WHERE rn = 2;

QUESTION ITEM 255 (PDF NUMBER 223)
QUESTION: Find employees with the highest salary in each job title.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH max_salary_per_job AS (
   SELECT job_title, MAX(salary) AS max_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY job_title
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN max_salary_per_job m ON e.job_title =
   m.job_title AND e.salary = m.max_salary;

  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id,
   SUM(CASE WHEN gender = 'M' THEN 1 ELSE 0
   END) * 1.0 / NULLIF(SUM(CASE WHEN gender = 'F'
   THEN 1 ELSE 0 END), 0) AS male_to_female_ratio
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id;

QUESTION ITEM 257 (PDF NUMBER 225)
QUESTION: Find customers who spent more than average in their country.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_spent_per_country AS (

     SELECT c.country, AVG(o.amount) AS avg_amount
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id = o.customer_id
   GROUP BY c.country
   )
   SELECT c.customer_id, SUM(o.amount) AS
   total_spent
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id = o.customer_id
   JOIN avg_spent_per_country a ON c.country =
   a.country
   GROUP BY c.customer_id, c.country, a.avg_amount
   HAVING SUM(o.amount) > a.avg_amount;

QUESTION ITEM 258 (PDF NUMBER 226)
QUESTION: Find employees who have not been assigned to any project in the last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   FROM employees e
   LEFT JOIN project_assignments pa ON e.id =
   pa.employee_id AND pa.assignment_date >=
   CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '1 year'
   WHERE pa.project_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 259 (PDF NUMBER 227)
QUESTION: Find the top 3 customers by total order amount in each region.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT region, customer_id, total_amount
   FROM (
   SELECT c.region, o.customer_id, SUM(o.amount) AS
   total_amount,

    ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   c.region ORDER BY SUM(o.amount) DESC) AS rn
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id = o.customer_id
   GROUP BY c.region, o.customer_id
   ) sub
   WHERE rn <= 3;

QUESTION ITEM 260 (PDF NUMBER 228)
QUESTION: Find employees hired after their managers.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   manager_name, e.hire_date, m.hire_date AS
   manager_hire_date

QUESTION ITEM 261 (PDF NUMBER 229)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered all products from a specific category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH category_products AS (
   SELECT product_id
   FROM products
   WHERE category_id = :category_id
   ),
   customer_products AS (
   SELECT customer_id, product_id
   FROM sales
   WHERE product_id IN (SELECT product_id FROM
   category_products)
   GROUP BY customer_id, product_id
   )

    SELECT customer_id
   FROM customer_products
   GROUP BY customer_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT product_id) = (SELECT
   COUNT(*) FROM category_products);

QUESTION ITEM 263 (PDF NUMBER 231)
QUESTION: Calculate the retention rate of customers monthover-month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_customers AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',

   order_date) AS month
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   ),
   retention AS (
   SELECT current.month AS current_month,
   current.customer_id
   FROM monthly_customers current
   JOIN monthly_customers previous ON
   current.customer_id = previous.customer_id

     AND current.month = previous.month + INTERVAL
   '1 month'
   )
   SELECT current_month, COUNT(DISTINCT
   customer_id) * 100.0 /
   (SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT customer_id)
   FROM monthly_customers WHERE month =
   current_month - INTERVAL '1 month') AS
   retention_rate
   FROM retention
   GROUP BY current_month
   ORDER BY current_month;

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

QUESTION ITEM 266 (PDF NUMBER 234)
QUESTION: Find products that were sold in every quarter of the current year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH quarterly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('quarter',
   sale_date) AS quarter
   FROM sales
   WHERE EXTRACT(YEAR FROM sale_date) =
   EXTRACT(YEAR FROM CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY product_id, quarter
   )
   SELECT product_id
   FROM quarterly_sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT quarter) = 4;

QUESTION ITEM 267 (PDF NUMBER 235)
QUESTION: Find customers whose orders decreased consecutively for 3 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   order_date) AS month, COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   ),
   orders_with_lag AS (
   SELECT customer_id, month, order_count,
   LAG(order_count, 1) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_1,
   LAG(order_count, 2) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_2

     FROM monthly_orders
   )
   SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders_with_lag
   WHERE order_count < prev_1 AND prev_1 < prev_2;

   FROM attendance

QUESTION ITEM 269 (PDF NUMBER 237)
QUESTION: Find the most common product combinations in orders (pairs).

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

TABLE: order_items
COLUMNS: order_item_id | order_id | product_id | quantity | unit_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 1001 | P1 | 1 | 80000
  2 | 1001 | P2 | 1 | 1500
  3 | 1002 | P4 | 1 | 7000
  4 | 1003 | P3 | 1 | 12000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH order_pairs AS (
   SELECT o1.order_id, o1.product_id AS product1,
   o2.product_id AS product2
   FROM order_items o1
   JOIN order_items o2 ON o1.order_id = o2.order_id
   AND o1.product_id < o2.product_id
   )
   SELECT product1, product2, COUNT(*) AS
   pair_count
   FROM order_pairs
   GROUP BY product1, product2
   ORDER BY pair_count DESC
   LIMIT 10;

QUESTION ITEM 270 (PDF NUMBER 238)
QUESTION: Find employees who have worked more than 40 hours in a week.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH weekly_hours AS (
   SELECT employee_id, DATE_TRUNC('week',
   work_date) AS week_start, SUM(hours_worked) AS
   total_hours
   FROM work_logs
   GROUP BY employee_id, week_start
   )
   SELECT employee_id, week_start, total_hours
   FROM weekly_hours
   WHERE total_hours > 40;

QUESTION ITEM 273 (PDF NUMBER 241)
QUESTION: Find products with an increasing sales trend over the last 3 months.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   WHERE sale_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '3 months'
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   sales_ranked AS (
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   LAG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_month_sales,
   LAG(total_sales, 2) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month) AS
   prev_2_month_sales
   FROM monthly_sales
   )
   SELECT DISTINCT product_id
   FROM sales_ranked
   WHERE total_sales > prev_month_sales AND
   prev_month_sales > prev_2_month_sales;

QUESTION ITEM 274 (PDF NUMBER 242)
QUESTION: Find departments where average salary is higher than the company average.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH company_avg AS (
   SELECT AVG(salary) AS avg_salary FROM
   employees
   )

    SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS dept_avg
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   HAVING AVG(salary) > (SELECT avg_salary FROM
   company_avg);

QUESTION ITEM 277 (PDF NUMBER 245)
QUESTION: Find employees who have not submitted their timesheets in the last month.

SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18

  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

   FROM employees e

   WHERE t.timesheet_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 280 (PDF NUMBER 248)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salaries are within 10% of their department's average salary.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH dept_avg AS (
   SELECT department_id, AVG(salary) AS avg_salary
   FROM employees
   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN dept_avg d ON e.department_id =
   d.department_id
   WHERE e.salary BETWEEN d.avg_salary * 0.9 AND
   d.avg_salary * 1.1;

QUESTION ITEM 281 (PDF NUMBER 249)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered the most products in each category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH product_totals AS (

     SELECT c.customer_id, p.category_id,
   SUM(s.quantity) AS total_quantity,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY p.category_id
   ORDER BY SUM(s.quantity) DESC) AS rank
   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id
   JOIN customers c ON s.customer_id = c.customer_id
   GROUP BY c.customer_id, p.category_id
   )
   SELECT customer_id, category_id, total_quantity
   FROM product_totals
   WHERE rank = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 282 (PDF NUMBER 250)
QUESTION: Find the top 5 longest projects.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT project_id, start_date, end_date,
   end_date - start_date AS duration
   FROM projects
   ORDER BY duration DESC
   LIMIT 5;

QUESTION ITEM 284 (PDF NUMBER 252)
QUESTION: Find the department with the most projects completed last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT department_id, COUNT(*) AS
   completed_projects
   FROM projects
   WHERE status = 'Completed' AND completion_date
   BETWEEN DATE_TRUNC('year',
   CURRENT_DATE) - INTERVAL '1 year' AND
   DATE_TRUNC('year', CURRENT_DATE) INTERVAL '1 day'
   GROUP BY department_id
   ORDER BY completed_projects DESC
   LIMIT 1;

QUESTION ITEM 285 (PDF NUMBER 253)
QUESTION: Find customers who have increased their order frequency month-over-month for 3

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
consecutive months.
   WITH monthly_orders AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   order_date) AS month, COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   ),
   orders_with_lag AS (
   SELECT customer_id, month, order_count,
   LAG(order_count, 1) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_1,
   LAG(order_count, 2) OVER (PARTITION BY
   customer_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_2
   FROM monthly_orders
   )

    SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM orders_with_lag
   WHERE order_count > prev_1 AND prev_1 > prev_2;

QUESTION ITEM 287 (PDF NUMBER 255)

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   FROM support_tickets

QUESTION ITEM 290 (PDF NUMBER 258)
QUESTION: Find products with sales revenue above the average revenue per product.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH avg_revenue AS (
   SELECT AVG(total_revenue) AS avg_rev
   FROM (
   SELECT product_id, SUM(amount) AS
   total_revenue
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   ) sub
   )
   SELECT product_id, SUM(amount) AS total_revenue

   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING SUM(amount) > (SELECT avg_rev FROM
   avg_revenue);

QUESTION ITEM 292 (PDF NUMBER 260)
QUESTION: Find employees who worked on all projects in the company.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

TABLE: project_assignments
COLUMNS: employee_id | project_id | start_date | end_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  3 | 501 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30
  4 | 501 | 2024-02-01 | 2025-06-30
  5 | 502 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28
  7 | 503 | 2025-01-01 | NULL

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH total_projects AS (
   SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT project_id) AS
   project_count FROM projects
   ),
   employee_projects AS (
   SELECT employee_id, COUNT(DISTINCT
   project_id) AS projects_worked
   FROM project_assignments
   GROUP BY employee_id
   )
   SELECT ep.employee_id
   FROM employee_projects ep
   JOIN total_projects tp ON 1=1
   WHERE ep.projects_worked = tp.project_count;

QUESTION ITEM 293 (PDF NUMBER 261)
QUESTION: Find customers who ordered products from all categories.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH category_count AS (
   SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT category_id) AS
   total_categories FROM products
   ),
   customer_categories AS (

     SELECT customer_id, COUNT(DISTINCT
   p.category_id) AS categories_ordered
   FROM sales s
   JOIN products p ON s.product_id = p.product_id
   GROUP BY customer_id
   )
   SELECT customer_id
   FROM customer_categories
   JOIN category_count ON 1=1
   WHERE categories_ordered = total_categories;

QUESTION ITEM 294 (PDF NUMBER 262)
QUESTION: Find the average tenure of employees by department.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15

QUESTION ITEM 295 (PDF NUMBER 263)
QUESTION: Find the number of orders placed on weekends vs weekdays.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT CASE
   WHEN EXTRACT(DOW FROM order_date) IN
   (0,6) THEN 'Weekend'
   ELSE 'Weekday'
   END AS day_type,
   COUNT(*) AS order_count
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY day_type;

QUESTION ITEM 296 (PDF NUMBER 264)
QUESTION: Find the percentage of orders with discounts per month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DATE_TRUNC('month', order_date) AS
   month,
   100.0 * SUM(CASE WHEN discount > 0 THEN 1
   ELSE 0 END) / COUNT(*) AS discount_percentage
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY month
   ORDER BY month;

QUESTION ITEM 297 (PDF NUMBER 265)
QUESTION: Find the employees who have never been late to work.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

TABLE: attendance
COLUMNS: employee_id | attendance_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  2 | 2025-01-01
  2 | 2025-01-02
  2 | 2025-01-03
  3 | 2025-01-01

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.id, e.name
   FROM employees e

   LEFT JOIN attendance a ON e.id = a.employee_id
   AND a.arrival_time > a.scheduled_start_time
   WHERE a.employee_id IS NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 298 (PDF NUMBER 266)
QUESTION: Find products with sales only during holiday seasons.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT product_id
   FROM sales s
   JOIN holidays h ON s.sale_date = h.holiday_date
   GROUP BY product_id
   HAVING COUNT(*) = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM
   sales WHERE product_id = s.product_id);

QUESTION ITEM 299 (PDF NUMBER 267)
QUESTION: Find the department with the largest increase in employee count compared to last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH current_year AS (

     SELECT department_id, COUNT(*) AS emp_count
   FROM employees
   WHERE hire_date <= CURRENT_DATE AND
   (termination_date IS NULL OR termination_date >=
   CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY department_id
   ),
   last_year AS (
   SELECT department_id, COUNT(*) AS emp_count
   FROM employees
   WHERE hire_date <= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year' AND (termination_date IS NULL
   OR termination_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year')
   GROUP BY department_id
   )
   SELECT c.department_id, c.emp_count COALESCE(l.emp_count,0) AS increase
   FROM current_year c
   LEFT JOIN last_year l ON c.department_id =
   l.department_id
   ORDER BY increase DESC
   LIMIT 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 300 (PDF NUMBER 268)
QUESTION: Find the average order value per customer segment.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   avg_order_value
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o ON c.customer_id = o.customer_id

     GROUP BY segment;

QUESTION ITEM 301 (PDF NUMBER 269)
QUESTION: Find employees who manage more than 3 projects.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

QUESTION ITEM 304 (PDF NUMBER 272)
QUESTION: Find employees whose salaries increased every year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH salary_diff AS (
   SELECT employee_id, year, salary,
   LAG(salary) OVER (PARTITION BY
   employee_id ORDER BY year) AS prev_salary
   FROM salaries
   )

    SELECT DISTINCT employee_id
   FROM salary_diff
   WHERE salary > prev_salary OR prev_salary IS NULL
   GROUP BY employee_id
   HAVING COUNT(*) = (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM
   salaries s2 WHERE s2.employee_id =

   salary_diff.employee_id);

QUESTION ITEM 305 (PDF NUMBER 273)
QUESTION: Find the total number of unique products sold in the last quarter.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT product_id) AS
   unique_products_sold
   FROM sales
   WHERE sale_date >= DATE_TRUNC('quarter',
   CURRENT_DATE) - INTERVAL '3 months'
   AND sale_date < DATE_TRUNC('quarter',
   CURRENT_DATE);

QUESTION ITEM 306 (PDF NUMBER 274)
QUESTION: Find the day with the highest sales in each month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH daily_sales AS (
   SELECT DATE(order_date) AS day, SUM(amount)
   AS total_sales
   FROM orders
   GROUP BY day
   ),
   ranked_sales AS (
   SELECT day, total_sales,
   RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY
   DATE_TRUNC('month', day) ORDER BY total_sales
   DESC) AS sales_rank

     FROM daily_sales
   )
   SELECT day, total_sales
   FROM ranked_sales
   WHERE sales_rank = 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 307 (PDF NUMBER 275)
QUESTION: Find the products with the highest sales increase compared to the previous month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   sales_diff AS (
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   LAG(total_sales) OVER (PARTITION BY
   product_id ORDER BY month) AS prev_month_sales
   FROM monthly_sales
   )
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales prev_month_sales AS increase
   FROM sales_diff
   WHERE prev_month_sales IS NOT NULL
   ORDER BY increase DESC
   LIMIT 10;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 308 (PDF NUMBER 276)
QUESTION: Find the top 5 customers by total order value in the last year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE

  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:

   total_order_value
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '1 year'

   LIMIT 5;

QUESTION ITEM 312 (PDF NUMBER 280)
QUESTION: Find products with the lowest average rating per category.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: product_reviews
COLUMNS: product_id | customer_id | rating | review_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | 101 | 4.5 | 2025-01-05
  P2 | 102 | 4.0 | 2025-01-06
  P3 | 103 | 4.8 | 2025-01-07

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT category_id, product_id, AVG(rating) AS

   avg_rating
   FROM product_reviews
   GROUP BY category_id, product_id
   QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION
   BY category_id ORDER BY AVG(rating)) = 1;

QUESTION ITEM 313 (PDF NUMBER 281)
QUESTION: Find employees who have never received a promotion.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15

QUESTION ITEM 314 (PDF NUMBER 282)
QUESTION: Find the total number of orders placed each day in the last week.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT DATE(order_date) AS order_day, COUNT(*)
   AS orders_count
   FROM orders
   WHERE order_date >= CURRENT_DATE INTERVAL '7 days'
   GROUP BY order_day
   ORDER BY order_day;

QUESTION ITEM 316 (PDF NUMBER 284)
QUESTION: Find the top 3 sales reps by number of deals closed this quarter.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT sales_rep_id, COUNT(*) AS deals_closed
   FROM sales_deals
   WHERE deal_close_date >= DATE_TRUNC('quarter',
   CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY sales_rep_id
   ORDER BY deals_closed DESC
   LIMIT 3;

QUESTION ITEM 318 (PDF NUMBER 286)
QUESTION: Find employees who report to a manager hired after them.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11

  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.id, e.name, m.id AS manager_id, m.name
   AS manager_name
   FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE e.hire_date < m.hire_date;

   FROM shipments
   GROUP BY shipping_method;

QUESTION ITEM 320 (PDF NUMBER 288)
QUESTION: Find orders where the total quantity exceeds 100.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

TABLE: order_items
COLUMNS: order_item_id | order_id | product_id | quantity | unit_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 1001 | P1 | 1 | 80000
  2 | 1001 | P2 | 1 | 1500
  3 | 1002 | P4 | 1 | 7000
  4 | 1003 | P3 | 1 | 12000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT order_id, SUM(quantity) AS total_quantity
   FROM order_items
   GROUP BY order_id
   HAVING SUM(quantity) > 100;

QUESTION ITEM 322 (PDF NUMBER 290)
QUESTION: Find products that have been ordered but never reviewed.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: products
COLUMNS: product_id | product_name | category_id | launch_date | list_price
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | Laptop | 1 | 2023-01-01 | 80000
  P2 | Mouse | 1 | 2023-02-01 | 1500
  P3 | Desk | 2 | 2024-01-15 | 12000
  P4 | Chair | 2 | 2024-02-01 | 7000
  P5 | Unused Product | 3 | 2025-01-01 | 3000

TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

TABLE: product_reviews
COLUMNS: product_id | customer_id | rating | review_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  P1 | 101 | 4.5 | 2025-01-05
  P2 | 102 | 4.0 | 2025-01-06
  P3 | 103 | 4.8 | 2025-01-07

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT p.product_id
   FROM products p
   LEFT JOIN product_reviews pr ON p.product_id =
   pr.product_id
   JOIN sales s ON p.product_id = s.product_id
   WHERE pr.review_id IS NULL
   GROUP BY p.product_id;

QUESTION ITEM 323 (PDF NUMBER 291)
QUESTION: Find employees who have worked on projects for more than 2 years.

SAMPLE ROWS:

  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20

  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12

SAMPLE ROWS:

QUESTION ITEM 324 (PDF NUMBER 292)
QUESTION: Find the product with the highest sales for each month.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: sales
COLUMNS: sale_id | customer_id | product_id | category_id | employee_id | sale_date | amount
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 101 | P1 | 1 | 5 | 2025-01-03 | 80000
  2 | 101 | P2 | 1 | 6 | 2025-01-03 | 1500
  3 | 102 | P3 | 2 | 5 | 2025-02-10 | 12000
  4 | 103 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-03-15 | 80000
  5 | 101 | P4 | 2 | 6 | 2025-03-20 | 7000
  6 | 104 | P2 | 1 | 5 | 2025-04-02 | 1500
  7 | 102 | P1 | 1 | 9 | 2025-04-12 | 80000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   sale_date) AS month, SUM(amount) AS total_sales
   FROM sales
   GROUP BY product_id, month
   ),
   ranked_sales AS (
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales,
   ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   month ORDER BY total_sales DESC) AS rn
   FROM monthly_sales
   )
   SELECT product_id, month, total_sales
   FROM ranked_sales
   WHERE rn = 1;

------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION ITEM 325 (PDF NUMBER 293)
QUESTION: Find customers with the highest order count in each region.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH customer_order_counts AS (
   SELECT c.region, o.customer_id, COUNT(*) AS
   order_count,

    ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY
   c.region ORDER BY COUNT(*) DESC) AS rn
   FROM customers c
   JOIN orders o

QUESTION ITEM 326 (PDF NUMBER 294)
QUESTION: Flag customers with an increase in orders every month this year.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: customers
COLUMNS: customer_id | name | region
SAMPLE ROWS:
  101 | Customer A | North
  102 | Customer B | South
  103 | Customer C | East
  104 | Customer D | West
  105 | No Purchase Customer | North

TABLE: orders
COLUMNS: order_id | customer_id | product_id | order_date | order_value | amount | region | discount_used
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1001 | 101 | P1 | 2025-01-03 | 81500 | 81500 | North | FALSE
  1002 | 101 | P4 | 2025-03-20 | 7000 | 7000 | North | TRUE
  1003 | 102 | P3 | 2025-02-10 | 12000 | 12000 | South | FALSE
  1004 | 103 | P1 | 2025-03-15 | 80000 | 80000 | East | FALSE
  1005 | 104 | P2 | 2025-04-02 | 1500 | 1500 | West | FALSE

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH monthly_counts AS (
   SELECT customer_id, DATE_TRUNC('month',
   order_date) AS month, COUNT(*) AS cnt
   FROM orders
   WHERE EXTRACT(YEAR FROM order_date) =
   EXTRACT(YEAR FROM CURRENT_DATE)
   GROUP BY customer_id, month
   ),
   increase_check AS (
   SELECT customer_id,
   LAG(cnt) OVER (PARTITION BY customer_id
   ORDER BY month) AS prev_cnt,
   cnt
   FROM monthly_counts
   )
   SELECT DISTINCT customer_id
   FROM increase_check
   WHERE cnt > prev_cnt AND prev_cnt IS NOT NULL;

QUESTION ITEM 327 (PDF NUMBER 295)
QUESTION: Find employees whose hire date is the same weekday as their manager's.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT e.id, e.name

    FROM employees e
   JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id
   WHERE EXTRACT(DOW FROM e.hire_date) =
   EXTRACT(DOW FROM m.hire_date);

   work_date) AS week_start, SUM(hours_worked) AS
   total_hours
   FROM work_logs
   GROUP BY employee_id, week_start;

QUESTION ITEM 329 (PDF NUMBER 297)
QUESTION: Identify suppliers who delivered to all regions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: table_name (illustrative placeholder)
Columns/rows are not specified by the PDF; define representative sample data for this question.

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH total_regions AS (
   SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT region) AS total FROM
   suppliers
   ),
   supplier_regions AS (
   SELECT supplier_id, COUNT(DISTINCT
   delivery_region) AS regions_served
   FROM deliveries
   GROUP BY supplier_id

   )
   SELECT supplier_id
   FROM supplier_regions
   JOIN total_regions ON regions_served = total;

QUESTION ITEM 331 (PDF NUMBER 299)
QUESTION: Retrieve employees with salary in top 5% company-wide.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: employees
COLUMNS: id | name | salary | department_id | manager_id | gender | job_title | hire_date
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | Asha | 120000 | 10 | NULL | F | Director | 2018-01-15
  2 | Ravi | 95000 | 10 | 1 | M | Manager | 2019-03-10
  3 | Meera | 85000 | 10 | 2 | F | Developer | 2020-06-01
  4 | Kiran | 85000 | 10 | 2 | M | Developer | 2021-02-18
  5 | Nikhil | 72000 | 20 | 1 | M | Salesperson | 2022-05-20
  6 | Divya | 68000 | 20 | 5 | F | Salesperson | 2023-07-11
  7 | Arun | 60000 | 30 | 1 | M | Analyst | 2021-09-01
  8 | Sara | 78000 | 40 | 1 | F | Analyst | 2020-11-30
  9 | Vijay | 52000 | 20 | 5 | M | Salesperson | 2024-01-12
  10 | Asha | 50000 | 30 | 7 | F | Analyst | 2024-04-02

ANSWER FROM PDF:
WITH salary_ranks AS (
   SELECT salary, PERCENT_RANK() OVER
   (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS pr
   FROM employees
   )
   SELECT e.*
   FROM employees e
   JOIN salary_ranks sr ON e.salary = sr.salary
   WHERE sr.pr <= 0.05;

QUESTION ITEM 332 (PDF NUMBER 300)
QUESTION: List departments with no open positions.

TABLES FOR THIS QUESTION:
TABLE: departments
COLUMNS: department_id | department_name | manager_id | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  10 | Engineering | NULL | 500000
  20 | Sales | NULL | 300000
  30 | HR | NULL | 150000
  40 | Finance | NULL | 250000
  50 | Empty Department | NULL | 50000

TABLE: projects
COLUMNS: id | project_id | project_name | department_id | manager_id | start_date | end_date | budget
SAMPLE ROWS:
  1 | 501 | Warehouse Modernization | 10 | 2 | 2024-01-01 | 2025-06-30 | 200000
  2 | 502 | CRM Upgrade | 20 | 5 | 2024-03-01 | 2025-02-28 | 100000
  3 | 503 | HR Portal | 30 | 7 | 2025-01-01 | NULL | 50000

ANSWER FROM PDF:
SELECT d.department_id
   FROM departments d
   LEFT JOIN job_openings j ON d.department_id =
   j.department_id AND j.status = 'Open'
   WHERE j.job_id IS NULL;
